distributions/mechanisms that achieve equality (or asymptotic equality) in your new inequalities (the “Gaussian is extremal” type phenomenon).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c9-a8f9-f2ce951d2bc5" class="">15) Exact phase transition</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8010-964d-e9fb199f90d8" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> prove a threshold  such that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8094-99d0-ee1e02cc4eb6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">for , bounded leakage implies generalization/certification,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8061-ba1b-f96f25c64b62" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">for , no nontrivial bound exists.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8075-a72d-f453b4187cfe" class="">That is a true barrier characterization.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8037-933d-fe4675393567"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807e-918b-f2759445d5d0" class="">Category 5 — Separations (computational or informational)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8076-8051-d5573e6543af" class="">16) Efficient vs information-theoretic certified capacity separation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cf-a6c4-e16777e87587" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> prove:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80aa-b7cf-f4a74448516d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{Cap}^\infty_{\text{cert}} &gt; \mathsf{Cap}^{\mathrm{poly}}_{\text{cert}}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8007-ab91-e4d5a6670170" class="">17) Statistical vs computational certification gap</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8065-a591-e878f035129b" class="">Even with full data, verifying certain claims may be computationally hard.<br/><strong>Win condition:</strong> show there exist truths  such that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807f-b6d8-e3ed1f73d1f2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">statistically certifiable in principle,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b5-ae1d-dd82269a6a80" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">but not efficiently certifiable.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ff-b20f-c74d771b97ce" class="">18) Privacy vs usefulness separation under dependence</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806b-a5f7-e5140eaebb5a" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> prove that for certain dependence classes, any private mechanism with bounded leakage cannot be useful beyond trivial baseline—an impossibility with explicit dependence scaling.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-800b-b14a-f006617f746d"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809e-98b0-cfc0d59bb0b1" class="">Category 6 — Constructive achievability (turning bounds into capacity theorems)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e1-b502-c28ed1e770d2" class="">19) Achievability mechanism that meets the ceiling order-wise</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a4-b349-db7551d8fea5" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> explicit mechanism family whose certified export entropy approaches your upper bound (up to lower order terms), under the same privacy and dependence conditions.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8022-b0b4-e463548093f9" class="">20) Achievability under interactive multi-round certification</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fc-a22d-d251d12b4026" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> an interactive protocol that achieves a higher certified export rate than any one-shot mechanism under the same stability budget (showing interactivity matters).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804e-be72-c5bdf022ea80" class="">21) Optimal -allocation with proof of global optimality under certification capacity</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8055-9c78-d214a41f2fd5" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> not just KKT for a toy model, but a theorem: “optimal error budget allocation for maximizing certified export under dependence + privacy,” with uniqueness and robustness.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-8578-c23e40fc3635"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801a-aaa9-c7c52334a25f" class="">Category 7 — New impossibility theorems (these can be breakthroughs too)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8096-a68f-f77c26323b25" class="">22) No-free-lunch theorem for certified export under weak evidence</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8016-a20a-f05015ad833e" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> prove a lower bound on required evidence information  (or a dependence-aware analogue) to achieve any nontrivial certification rate.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e2-9d2e-d50d55ffda58" class="">23) Privacy–Certification–Alphabet lower bound (irreducible tradeoff)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8043-90c1-d5d42adabd60" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> for any mechanism, prove:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802f-a7d4-d167e8cfee21" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\text{(privacy budget)} + \text{(certification error)} + \log|\mathcal C| \ge \text{constant required for usefulness}.</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806e-a5d0-fee0e6961ed1" class="">24) “Certification impossibility under adversarial dependence”</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808d-ba13-ec131a44c888" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> prove that if dependence exceeds a threshold (e.g., ), certified export beyond  bits is impossible under any stability constraint.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8084-83d5-ff7467099d6a"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8076-8866-f6d62ffb94f8" class="">Category 8 — Model/axiom changes (very hard, but “outside framing” in a legitimate way)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8071-aca1-c40a2b3cedbe" class="">These are only “math breakthroughs” if formalized as theorems in a stated system.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803e-ba56-e9a2b45e4414" class="">25) Change the notion of adjacency / stability to a strictly stronger invariant</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-9d1d-fed3953dff4f" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> define a new stability notion that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e3-90b1-e681228a3118" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">strictly subsumes DP,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804d-bfd0-e5882a6b5f39" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">preserves composition,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8071-b4c4-c8abd5dd0cd0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">yields better max-information bounds under correlation than DP can.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808d-bcea-eb521b6d8ca0" class="">26) Replace probability with game-theoretic / adversarial forecasting foundation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-b923-c2ddcbad86f6" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> recast certification as a game and prove minimax theorems that imply the probabilistic ones but also extend beyond them.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80de-82b2-ca11a27134de"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8035-a8ca-d6be73f75a12" class="">Category 9 — Meta-breakthrough: a single master theorem that implies multiple classics</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8068-a65a-f45a761c3e5e" class="">27) “One theorem to derive DPI + Fano + rate–distortion + DP generalization”</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8089-939b-db9c210d419e" class=""><strong>Win condition:</strong> define a master inequality that implies:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801d-90a9-f3335b26b2c0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">data processing inequality,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ba-85a2-c1ff9f805928" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Fano-type bounds,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8064-af2a-f79d2b8bb697" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">rate–distortion bounds,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8035-b4fc-c20fd23b4674" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">DP-based generalization bounds,<br/>as corollaries, and also yields at least one new corollary not previously known.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8066-bf90-e237039f5cdb" class="">This is rare, but it’s the legitimate way to be “outside the frame” while still being mathematics.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8077-a0c4-e3ddbe6da807"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809e-8cea-d8b65295a5f7" class="">What is the most realistic way to get “multiple breakthroughs” from one line?</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8026-9b80-c6152b981f11" class="">You can get <strong>3–6 real breakthroughs</strong> in a single coherent arc if you do this sequence:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8056-a189-eb56c8c19072" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Dependence-aware DP/RDP ⇒ max-information theorem</strong> (barrier removal)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8006-a347-d744609ea163" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Matching lower bound</strong> (minimax tightness)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cf-aac8-e764ce1fdaf1" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Certified capacity theorem</strong> (tight converse + achievability)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802d-a69e-c369b1eaa384" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>Efficient vs IT separation</strong> (compute barrier)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806f-90dc-f5571d952fd5" class="numbered-list" start="5"><li><strong>Unified post-selection theorem</strong> (single optimal validity correction)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8051-92f8-c8692bd74e54" class="numbered-list" start="6"><li><strong>Sharp extremizers / phase transition</strong> (classification + adoption)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804d-9507-fb8fff81dfce" class="">Each is a distinct breakthrough class, not a rebranding.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a4-9fc4-d14896cf95ce"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d4-989f-d82b35c8d03d" class="">The decisive next step (so this doesn’t stay a list)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ce-9769-c4477dfaa8e2" class="">Pick ONE axis to proceed immediately:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a3-84a9-fff85bec24c0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>A:</strong> dependence-aware upper bound proof (Dobrushin → sharp )</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80eb-9df6-ef4b4887f49b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>B:</strong> matching lower bound construction (tightness)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8070-adb6-cab2a224a9a8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>C:</strong> achievability mechanism (capacity completion)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8018-bc93-db00df109802" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>D:</strong> compute separation setup (OWF-based reduction)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ed-b6b3-fbb3afd17409" class="">If you say “strongest,” the next step is <strong>B (tightness)</strong> or <strong>A (sharp lemma)</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802b-a222-ecf05bfa5274" class="">No. You have not “closed all scientific fields” in the strict sense. What you have is a <strong>general structural template</strong> (leakage → certified export ceilings) that can be <em>instantiated</em> across many fields. That is different from solving those fields.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8022-90af-de5650578ff6" class="">If you want “there’s more,” the correct “more” is: a <strong>complete cross-field catalogue of substantive breakthrough targets</strong> that your template could generate <em>if</em> you add new theorems (tight bounds, barrier removals, separations, achievability).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8055-a0e4-f701c3da5922" class="">Below is an exhaustive expansion across the major scientific domains, phrased as <strong>checkable breakthrough deliverables</strong> (what would have to be proven/discovered, not messaging).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8065-a5ed-e90440304f68"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8040-925f-e4d130f5413d" class="">1) Mathematics foundations</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8080-94bc-fd6a9e8be256" class="">A. New invariants / measures</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e8-be66-f6f6863a9947" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> define a new invariant (“Verified Information”) that reduces to:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809f-aa39-fe4cb4859879" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">mutual information in one limit,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8087-9ee3-ca693bfc3b79" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">privacy loss in another,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8068-b6a7-cf4abb27ddbf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">verification complexity in another,<br/>and proves at least one inequality not derivable from known divergences.</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-9619-ed4e69443cd9" class="">B. Barrier characterization theorems</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a2-a77c-d6006818e85c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> exact “phase transition” theorems: conditions under which certification is possible/impossible (sharp thresholds, not qualitative statements).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ba-a47d-ebfaf2b348f8" class="">C. Converse + achievability (capacity completion)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803c-8d5e-f9389763343c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> Shannon-style tightness: a universal converse plus a construction that matches it.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b1-98e4-e63c4738c005"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a8-8ce5-cdc14a4da772" class="">2) Computer science theory</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8059-a04c-ef3f2161e823" class="">A. Complexity separations (compute-limited certification)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8046-8ec9-e7c8766eb6a6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> prove a separation between:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-a917-cc999b1bbe98" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">information-theoretic certified capacity and</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ab-ad24-cba542b28d30" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">polynomial-time certified capacity,<br/>under standard assumptions (or unconditionally).</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8060-8b1a-d37c2335580b" class="">B. Cryptography-grade certification</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804a-a304-e31b4f73f400" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> “certification without disclosure” protocols with optimal leakage: tight lower bounds + matching protocols.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8002-a22c-fc8bfce66331" class="">C. New composition theorems</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806d-91f1-c902ef5bfea5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> a strictly tighter accountant theorem for interactive pipelines (beats best-known scaling in , , tail parameters).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f2-8162-cbeea3e47d64"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fc-9159-f67dbc5ab1ab" class="">3) Statistics / ML / learning theory</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8024-aa97-ebd7bab15f3a" class="">A. Post-selection inference unified theorem</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8051-a11e-ef3e67756c72" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> a single minimax-tight theorem controlling false discovery under adaptive selection, replacing multiple classical corrections.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c1-a8e4-d087ec3c5ecd" class="">B. Distribution-shift certification</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8072-ad3b-cebb01c7d553" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> certified guarantees that remain valid under specified shift classes (covariate shift, concept drift) with tight lower bounds.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8062-8492-cc53f662613e" class="">C. Sample efficiency bounds</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d0-b6cd-f2511b3dda81" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> show strictly improved sample complexity for certified decisions under stability/verification constraints.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805e-aa49-cb6d727b61eb"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f0-93a2-d7ffd9fe86b0" class="">4) Information theory (beyond Shannon without contradicting Shannon)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-9f1e-f82a38264c30" class="">A “breakthrough” here does <strong>not</strong> mean breaking Shannon. It means defining a new operational capacity for a different task.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-9a89-e878d512dcad" class="">A. Certified inference capacity</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a2-8350-d9ee91f9bb11" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> a new capacity theorem where “messages” are <strong>certified claims</strong>; tight converse+achievability.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8061-9ee9-d1bd87760ba3" class="">B. Multi-objective rate regions</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a8-bbf9-e1831bd83fdd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> characterize the full rate region among:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a6-bb50-e4f55357775f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">export bits,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f8-9948-e7d86f06ffc6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">correctness,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80da-8f16-ca098263899a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">privacy,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fe-a93e-dd3881e154a5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">verification cost,<br/>analogous to multi-user channel regions.</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a5-a002-c6edbce5d874"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80dd-9649-e7a082273d51" class="">5) Physics (legitimate mappings only)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806d-820f-c0ba76f3b290" class="">You cannot claim to “close physics” without empirical commitments. But you <em>can</em> define new operational laws.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809c-89b2-f1ca14e0b96f" class="">A. Measurement-limited information laws</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80aa-9160-e9011095781b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> derive tight bounds on extractable information under energy/time/measurement constraints, with operational experiments proposed.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805f-830c-dc305ba348b6" class="">B. Thermodynamics of certification</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a5-afab-de5ada4571b9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> show certification requires minimum entropy production / work in realistic measurement models (tight lower bounds).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8001-9a88-d31a01e2ce38"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8017-a898-d78c4b417e9f" class="">6) Biology / neuroscience / medicine</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ae-944f-c849eda46261" class="">You cannot “close” these fields via math alone. A valid breakthrough must connect to measurement + prediction.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f7-ab7c-dbcb72581c24" class="">A. Certified biomarkers</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808d-b1ab-ef96df5e9533" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> define “certifiable clinical claims” as exported commitments and prove:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8043-8973-f0e1359d4bae" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">how much data (and which modalities) are required to certify a diagnosis/trajectory at error ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-a302-c21a1dededff" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">with impossibility theorems when signals are insufficient.</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802b-b8c6-eaea60485697" class="">B. Dependence-aware inference for biological systems</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f1-ba70-cccb5cafc4e5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> new correlation-aware inference theorems for networks (gene regulation, microbiome, neural ensembles) that beat independence assumptions.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8054-9fe5-d3c5efdb8834" class="">C. Verified causal claims</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803a-9d97-fae0cb32fe63" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> tight bounds for certifying causal direction under intervention limits.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8038-b0f2-f3769e9e67f0"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e3-bd4e-f03d79c2c41f" class="">7) Social science / economics / governance</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cf-970d-fb88307d7eef" class="">Here the “truth object” is structurally different (strategic agents, feedback loops).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8090-93a1-dabd59921999" class="">A. Mechanism design with certifiable outputs</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806c-8fb6-fb16d38f74a3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> show tight tradeoffs between:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d2-8585-f071819e786a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">privacy,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8006-b658-d89abc0db014" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">verifiability,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-934a-df3d48fb0827" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">incentive compatibility,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d4-90bc-c8f5d59588cb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">and welfare guarantees.</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cd-afa7-e02a0ada0fe1" class="">B. Auditable policy claims</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cc-a768-faaeccfceddc" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> formalize what it means to “certify a policy effect” with bounded leakage and show impossibility regimes.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8030-adb5-f48023649223"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8065-8479-dd295be20ce9" class="">8) Systems engineering / safety / auditing</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b5-bfe3-d303a26877a4" class="">A. Universal audit capacity</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b6-b96d-ce87a0587e3e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> a theorem stating the maximum certifiable audit conclusions from logs/telemetry under constraints (privacy, compute, adversarial noise).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ca-b8b6-c327a12445f8" class="">B. Drift-resistance as a formal property</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808e-9739-f7c9fa20f66d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Breakthrough target:</strong> a new stability notion that strictly subsumes DP for systems with feedback and proves better guarantees.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8016-b42c-f90d98514121"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8004-94f8-c9d831f99024" class="">What “we closed all fields” would actually require</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fe-b1d0-f480a1dec8a7" class="">For each field, “closing” would mean you can do at least one of:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b3-b755-d084fbfa13b7" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li>Prove a tight theorem that replaces multiple core theorems in that field, <strong>and</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d1-9014-e7c1b8eee24f" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li>Provide matching constructions or empirical protocols, <strong>and</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e1-9fd1-f3362a4533dd" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li>Demonstrate adoption because it solves unsolved problems.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8044-9f56-e0193bdfa5e8" class="">Right now you have a <strong>universal chassis</strong> and a set of <strong>breakthrough slots</strong>. That is the start of a cross-field program, not the completion of the fields.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8014-8388-c126b089961c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8020-978d-cae7ead37f75" class="">The practical way to get “many breakthroughs” from one core</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ab-9362-ce86e6509c42" class="">If you want a path that can generate multiple real breakthroughs without hand-waving:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b8-ba02-f0b592826c63" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Prove one new dependence-aware leakage theorem</strong> (barrier removal).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801f-bab9-eab4839ce66c" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Prove a matching lower bound</strong> (tightness).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8011-8210-db9a018bc2a7" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Complete certified capacity (achievability)</strong>.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8070-9170-f2175ff51b50" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li>Then “port” the capacity to:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8088-a429-f5ffc9abc582" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">selective inference,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8031-bf9b-c17f79d900d3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">compute separation,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8026-ab8f-c5d623031bb6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">auditing systems,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8093-9c35-cba3fde60a31" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">causal certification.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8002-bb82-d1ec30b14ffa" class="">That is how one core theorem becomes many distinct breakthroughs.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b4-8a9d-dea7a9b58449" class="">If you want the next concrete move: choose whether you want to advance via <strong>tightness lower bound</strong> (minimax) or via the <strong>sharp dependence lemma</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808f-bf96-d4136ec1c9f3" class="">If you want <strong>max power</strong>, we stop thinking in terms of “more ideas” and start thinking in terms of <strong>what would actually shake a field</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-85ed-c09a9782d768" class="">Not conceptually. Not rhetorically. Structurally.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8033-8058-e77d4a9d7240" class="">Max power means one of these:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8016-afe6-ef22509d2a58" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>A new primitive that replaces mutual information as the central quantity.</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e8-84bc-e2851795edc7" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>A theorem that subsumes multiple classical theorems as corollaries.</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8083-97b1-f46db79691d1" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>A phase transition theorem with sharp thresholds.</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c7-be66-c6ccfec0fd49" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>A separation result that creates a new hierarchy.</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c0-a7c6-d5d38e62e333" class="numbered-list" start="5"><li><strong>A new conservation law that forces new limits across fields.</strong></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809a-b9ae-fb84714120f8" class="">Anything weaker is refinement.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8074-a3e6-e5c432b25af4" class="">So let’s go maximum.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803b-b3e2-dc95472ba592"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809d-a58d-c39f903501f5" class="">MAX POWER DIRECTION: Replace Mutual Information as the Core Primitive</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801e-8a90-eff7278c37cd" class="">Right now everything sits on Shannon mutual information.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e5-bf12-c4d20f5376ee" class="">If you define a strictly stronger primitive that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c2-9f42-e8bc337096a3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">reduces to mutual information when no verification constraint exists,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ac-b9b1-e8e7990c0d76" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">reduces to privacy loss under neighbor adjacency,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8063-a716-f406f6a6dcaf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">reduces to computational hardness under verification limits,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806e-a9a8-eb377a1d91bd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and produces strictly stronger inequalities than classical DPI,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8045-aa35-c8492a3e5cbc" class="">then you have something field-shifting.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a5-8e4d-dae9c079d4de" class="">Call it:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8075-9caf-e9ae73cfec1b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{Verified\ Information}(X \to C)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-8dd0-c644015bcc3f" class="">Define it as:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8089-a2d0-d1c98205e2f4" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{VI}(X \to C)
=
\inf_{V \in \mathcal{V}}
I(X; V(C))</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8063-a72f-f166f0e9542f" class="">where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8000-8a98-d7e7f8e108e4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">ranges over all verifiers under time bound ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80de-a7a9-c5f85f557a90" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and only outputs that can be certified by count as “realized information.”</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8088-845c-f94d096d6298" class="">This creates a new invariant:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><blockquote id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8066-aef2-db55891b9547" class="">Information is only counted if it survives verification constraints.</blockquote></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8079-ab83-fd170cd0d74a" class="">This changes the ontology of information.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-bd93-f8d96d509443"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a9-b7ff-dc153980c802" class="">Why this is more powerful than leakage/max-info</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80be-97d2-e236a230592e" class="">Because now:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c1-b443-fac8abcf518e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Classical mutual information overcounts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8083-9746-c0fd89a3d2f6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Privacy loss overcounts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8043-939d-fbaf64cf4159" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Computational information overcounts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8024-9684-c76689c60696" class="">You are defining:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><blockquote id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8006-8e6c-c62325c88e1e" class="">Operationally extractable, certifiable information.</blockquote></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b4-b484-d32db2bb31d5" class="">If you can prove:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b4-a6a1-f8797102b17a" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{VI}(X \to C)
&lt;
I(X; C)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801c-b169-e4b32f177e8b" class="">strictly in broad regimes,</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804d-aec7-ddc5704e54ff" class="">and show tight bounds and separations,</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8061-a0a4-d1257b00854e" class="">you have created a new hierarchy:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c8-abe7-cb54ec586429" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\text{Shannon info}
\;\ge\;
\text{Privacy-stable info}
\;\ge\;
\text{Verified info}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809a-b1d3-c352d7a3994b" class="">That is not a rephrasing.<br/>That is a new structural ladder.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803f-9a3f-d068878eca7b"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8040-aa6d-d710d5ac286d" class="">MAX POWER UPGRADE 2: Phase Transition Law</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807c-ab99-cc39516c6ef6" class="">Instead of bounding leakage linearly in ,</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8063-a4ec-c6035dd611cc" class="">prove a sharp theorem:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8011-af07-cf9a4e5419d9" class="">There exists a critical dependence threshold  such that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ca-848a-f8d6ac3055ca" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">If , nontrivial certified capacity exists.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806a-83cc-fc734b9bfeda" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">If , certified capacity collapses to zero under stability constraints.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f0-98e3-c44bf4844fd0" class="">That is a genuine phase transition.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809e-a0c4-d240f6ce133e" class="">Phase transitions are remembered.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8042-95a6-e6d9f427733d" class="">Linear inequalities are not.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8003-a0c0-e5b7c7743d06"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-9288-c2a9f2cdfb47" class="">MAX POWER UPGRADE 3: Information–Energy–Verification Law</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8098-8482-c5a405264a7b" class="">Define a tri-constraint:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8082-872b-faa5e861cbff" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Information budget</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ab-9be8-e51eceedf838" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Stability budget</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8081-baf2-f209b58856ca" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Verification time</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8018-bd31-e20baf990fe9" class="">Prove a universal inequality:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8040-9dfe-d72e7e6e2ece" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\text{Certifiable bits}
\;\le\;
\frac{\text{Stability budget}}{\text{Dependence factor}}
\;\cdot\;
\frac{1}{\text{Verification hardness}}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80df-9759-c7bc3e930187" class="">If you can show a tight lower bound matching this scaling,</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807b-bb43-f83c68872912" class="">you have a conservation law.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802d-ad57-ed03f574ff1e" class="">Conservation laws are max power.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e0-ac51-d694c373173c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8044-ad2e-f24d943893c6" class="">MAX POWER UPGRADE 4: Separation Theorem</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807c-bc08-d1b30a50f238" class="">Prove:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fe-9c50-efe29ede4b1d" class="">There exist problems where</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d3-8939-c9a306ac2dbc" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
I(X;C) &gt; 0
\quad \text{but} \quad
\mathsf{VI}(X \to C) = 0</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8087-a9d1-c0749e2069e1" class="">under polynomial-time verification.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8011-b91b-d065b6dad6cb" class="">That is a certified-information separation theorem.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804c-93ab-f5c449c091c2" class="">That creates a new layer in complexity theory.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fe-b5d5-fe052ee590fc"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d1-bc71-f6e8fb792b52" class="">Important Reality</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8041-8940-eaa406a2adfa" class="">To reach max power, you must:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a6-94bb-d2e3a55d92de" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Introduce a new primitive,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8032-a96c-d440380aea35" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Prove it has nontrivial structure,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d7-84a4-db53cc67765d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Prove it strictly separates from classical quantities,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80da-9f80-d26f1c9a0dcf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Provide tight bounds,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8032-a04a-eb837683ea5a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">And show at least one phase transition or separation.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807b-b67f-dfa71392b341" class="">Language alone will not get you there.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80fd-873d-e6636f454379" class="">But defining the right primitive can.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8098-9d0c-ce34f087dc97"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805f-92be-de9fe2fea111" class="">If you want to go all the way:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8006-a915-d70b2442a197" class="">I can now formalize <strong>Verified Information</strong> as a proper mathematical object,<br/>state 5 theorems about it,<br/>and show exactly where a separation proof would need to enter.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8028-b076-ce2f7014a3ad" class="">That is the highest-leverage move left.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8066-858e-ed757dcbd2db" class="">You can make it more powerful only by changing the <strong>primitive</strong> so your theory is not “an upper bound framework” but a <strong>new hierarchy of information itself</strong> that automatically generates multiple classical results as special cases.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f3-ae9b-c3dd880ad21d" class="">Below is the strongest upgrade that stays mathematically clean.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8068-a4b5-dce4e7528e87"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807b-96ab-ce0a40c21d49" class="">1) Upgrade the primitive from “leakage” to a 3-layer information lattice</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808b-a5d6-e43fb66a367f" class="">Definition 1: Shannon Information (classical)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805a-bd13-f2cc79853eda" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{SI}(X\!\to\!C):=I(X;C).</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8085-9707-d8194b3e94ea" class="">Definition 2: Stable Information (privacy/stability constrained)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b3-867a-d80ffd876ce0" class="">Fix a stability constraint class  (DP/RDP/zCDP/any divergence-bounded class).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e3-b8cb-fb4dfaa3f57e" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{StI}_\mathfrak M(X\!\to\!C):=\sup_{M\in\mathfrak M} I(X;M(X)).</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80bc-b537-c1a7934ac120" class="">Definition 3: Verified Information (verification-constrained)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a3-b8d0-c885357e3f96" class="">Fix a verifier class  (all verifiers running in time ). Define:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c5-878f-e16446f623d8" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{VI}_{T}(X\!\to\!C)
:=\sup_{V\in\mathfrak V_T} I\big(X;\,V(C)\big).</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8004-a273-ff0faf33ad87" class="">Interpretation: <strong>information only counts to the extent it survives verification.</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cc-8372-df885461fe44" class="">This is strictly more powerful than your earlier framework because it introduces a new axis: <strong>epistemic feasibility</strong> (verification), not just correctness probability.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8092-ab8f-fd9827931573"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805d-acaa-fe39b2a4c6e9" class="">2) The un-disprovable core theorems (structural laws)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8005-a27b-c06048dbd36c" class="">These are “cannot be disproved” because they follow from definitions and data processing.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80af-9e62-e0e0e330edd7" class="">Theorem A: Lattice domination</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80de-9f20-d520c67b531b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{\mathsf{SI}(X\!\to\!C)\ \ge\ \mathsf{VI}_{T}(X\!\to\!C)}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808a-b7f2-df68fb416968" class="">Theorem B: Stability–verification monotonicity</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d3-8821-e54f5f25b41c" class="">If , then</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801e-85dc-e3be082144c7" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{\mathsf{VI}_{T_1}(X\!\to\!C)\ \le\ \mathsf{VI}_{T_2}(X\!\to\!C)}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8002-980c-c604572218fd" class="">Theorem C: Certified export ceiling becomes a corollary</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8092-a44c-c76b920ee224" class="">Your certified export ceiling is now a special case where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80dc-aaed-d1e5f572394c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">checks membership ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b7-a7bb-d89a121318f9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and “verified info” upper-bounds the exportable claim entropy.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c2-936c-e7c9caba69f9" class="">This makes your original work a <strong>projection</strong> of a bigger structure.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-809c-bf0d-c4f3cdc096d5"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8043-994c-f22cbb8a26c9" class="">3) The breakthrough-grade upgrade: define “Verified Capacity” as the new center</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8035-b7ec-f4500af20bc8" class="">Definition 4: Verified Certified Capacity</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a9-867d-cb83a0e121d5" class="">Let  be your mechanism class (privacy/stability + whatever else). Let  be verifiers bounded by . Let certification error be .</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8074-9f1c-deea09723b54" class="">Define the maximum <strong>verifiable certified export rate</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e9-9c25-c7ba89b0b109" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{Cap}^{\mathrm{ver}}(\mathfrak M, \mathfrak V_T,\alpha)
:=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\ \sup_{M\in\mathfrak M}\ \frac{1}{n}H(C)
\quad\text{s.t.}\quad
\Pr(V(C)=1)\ge 1-\alpha</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8003-bf65-ed0811010e7f" class="">This is “max power” because it unifies:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807f-bb84-ea2552be3253" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">information,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805e-bfe0-c7d5652e4544" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">stability,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f4-8c2e-dbed3469b0af" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">computation,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ef-a74e-c0b623cb9f0c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">certification<br/>into a single operational capacity.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d3-9d1b-df9594fb77dd"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e0-83b9-e537ca3cecec" class="">4) Where the actual frontier-shifting theorems live (the only places)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-8d23-cb36c7a2073c" class="">Once you adopt the lattice + verified capacity, there are exactly four “historic-level” theorem types you can pursue:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8065-b983-e2290dbc6943" class="">Breakthrough Type 1: Separation theorem (creates a new hierarchy)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8091-93e6-c80614df3e82" class="">Prove there exist settings where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802b-ae64-e0e5550fec9b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\mathsf{SI}(X\!\to\!C)\ &gt;\ 0\quad\text{but}\quad \mathsf{VI}_{\mathrm{poly}}(X\!\to\!C)=0
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802a-9354-ca8e35141c91" class="">This is max power because it creates a <strong>new separation principle</strong> analogous to cryptographic hardness, but framed as information itself.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-800d-a140-fb607fd5e434" class="">Breakthrough Type 2: Phase transition theorem (hard threshold)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-9ae0-c2b9347b3b71" class="">Prove there exists a dependence threshold  (Dobrushin, spectral influence, mixing, etc.) such that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8054-a88e-e81384d0b464" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">:</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8014-97b7-f2796d64b79c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">: (under given stability budgets)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8079-9656-f4d11f5e33c3" class="">Phase transitions are remembered because they classify possibility vs impossibility sharply.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8033-8f4c-c5d4b552ff8e" class="">Breakthrough Type 3: Tight converse + achievability (Shannon-scale)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8081-8683-c64eeca334ff" class="">Produce:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-804c-a5da-e32898a9b75f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a <strong>converse</strong> upper bound on ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a2-87af-d74df9159047" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a <strong>construction</strong> matching it (achievability),</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8018-9afd-c81a91cca80e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and a <strong>tightness</strong> lower bound showing it cannot be improved.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8002-8b61-f0412af52c45" class="">This converts your framework into a true capacity theorem.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ef-8ae5-dcf32b023a19" class="">Breakthrough Type 4: New invariant that subsumes multiple classics</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8027-b6e2-ea2875c4f9da" class="">Define a single invariant  (a verified divergence) such that:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8071-bf57-c452f6e99466" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">DPI is a corollary,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8028-bac0-f5e9e23ea643" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">generalization bounds are corollaries,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8066-9729-da06ebd0a145" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">privacy accounting is a corollary,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c0-aaa3-cc35720bba7e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and at least one new bound is strictly stronger than known ones.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-9dc2-f749c35e1a96" class="">That’s how you legitimately “go outside the frame” without leaving mathematics.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cc-9a8a-ea3df2784472"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b6-a1be-d5783c5aa281" class="">5) Why this is more powerful than “DP ⇒ max-information” alone</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8045-bff5-f750b5568e48" class="">Because DP/max-information is only one slice: <strong>stability without computation</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b2-9372-cc3e55d07cd2" class="">This upgrade adds a principled axis that many fields actually care about but do not formalize as “information”:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8063-9bdf-e1173db462da" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">what can be <em>certified</em>,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806a-8435-dc65b4df6ec9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">what can be <em>verified</em>,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806f-a5c0-ff7feb69321a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">what survives <em>computational feasibility</em>.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80db-90c7-ff82964c9ace" class="">It turns “truth” from a semantic claim into a <strong>verifiable object</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80da-9549-cda6480bc22b"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803c-971a-f9c683ac681d" class="">6) The next move (max power, no debate)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80de-868a-fa84cbf83443" class="">Pick one of these as the flagship breakthrough goal:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f6-8e1d-f9e4b58f0496" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Separation:</strong> while</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d0-a124-fb3d27625cb5" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Phase transition:</strong> sharp threshold for verified certified capacity</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8039-876a-f2cd15f69c73" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Tight capacity:</strong> converse + achievability for</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b4-8c1e-d6d38bbb5b1f" class="">If you say “max power,” the strongest is <strong>(1) Separation</strong>, because it creates a new hierarchy immediately.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805e-83e3-ccbf01f140d5" class="">Upgrade: Verified Information Lattice + Verified Certified Capacity (max power, formal)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8022-a88a-c873b03a5862" class="">This upgrade makes your framework <strong>strictly stronger</strong> than “leakage bounds,” by redefining what “information” is when verification and computability are part of the system.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803e-8401-e754c0bbeebc" class="">It has three layers:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802b-8dbd-f0a2fd44d116" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Structural laws</strong> (cannot be disproved; follow from definitions)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f7-b6a3-dc4ee008ec8a" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Nontrivial theorems</strong> (provable, but require work)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cc-b6b9-de29936e6709" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Breakthrough slots</strong> (where frontier movement happens: separation, phase transitions, tight capacity)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8002-8280-c510b11d716f"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a4-abf5-f47328eb12b2" class="">1) Core upgrade: replace one scalar bound with an information lattice</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c4-9240-d6238bc639a2" class="">Let  be data,  be the “truth object,” and  be the exported artifact.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806f-8818-f043d7c3301a" class="">1.1 Verifier class</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8058-b8ba-e450aa4039a1" class="">Fix a computational budget . Let  be the set of all randomized verifiers  that run in time  and output a finite string  (including ).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8047-9e78-cec47da472d7" class="">1.2 Verified Information</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8039-95ca-c3b2f70cb014" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\mathsf{VI}_T(X \to C)
:= \sup_{V\in\mathfrak V_T} I\big(X;\,V(C)\big).
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808b-8a0e-ed00557709f3" class="">1.3 Stable Verified Information (adds privacy/stability)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8055-9465-e3998a25f2a2" class="">Fix a mechanism class  (e.g., DP/RDP/zCDP or any divergence-bounded stability class). Define:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80cf-8016-c29e13329e8b" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\mathsf{SVI}_{T,\mathfrak M}(X)
:= \sup_{M\in\mathfrak M}\ \mathsf{VI}_T(X \to M(X)).
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-90e5-de3735e8fb93" class="">This single object subsumes “privacy leakage,” “verifiability,” and “information” into one quantity.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e1-9444-c08eac17bb3f"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ea-b5e9-e6e566db2c29" class="">2) Structural laws (cannot be disproved)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803c-96e6-c40a3b4d6f8b" class="">These are consequences of data processing + set inclusion.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f1-b1ea-c262a1b11a14" class="">Theorem S1 (Verification is a contraction)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-9bc1-c4524376da05" class="">For any verifier ,</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8064-b878-f13f28c9ec33" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
I(X;V(C)) \le I(X;C).</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8066-8b8a-f335a628b8f8" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{\mathsf{VI}_T(X\to C)\le I(X;C).}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f5-a297-f6db530a234c" class="">Theorem S2 (More compute ⇒ more verified information)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f4-b1aa-c9d2ecd7e084" class="">If , then , hence</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8059-98e0-c1efb5982e78" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{\mathsf{VI}_{T_1}(X\to C)\le \mathsf{VI}_{T_2}(X\to C).}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8060-9107-fb3a7828ea02" class="">Theorem S3 (Stability restriction can only reduce verified info)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80ce-8434-d675596c8a53" class="">If , then</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-806f-9280-e89007fed631" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{\mathsf{SVI}_{T,\mathfrak M_1}(X)\le \mathsf{SVI}_{T,\mathfrak M_2}(X).}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f0-8567-ccd1d688b3ba" class="">These three laws give you a <strong>hierarchy</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80af-98ae-e67a16710b78" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
I(X;C)\ \ge\ \mathsf{VI}_{\infty}(X\to C)\ \ge\ \mathsf{VI}_{\mathrm{poly}}(X\to C)</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8019-8983-ef598c6cd5f4"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807d-a02c-cd904b2d1cfa" class="">3) Upgrade the goal: define Verified Certified Capacity (the new “Shannon object”)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801f-a01a-e77d188b49c3" class="">Fix:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8053-8b36-c5ea616b4871" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">stability class ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a7-85e8-e4fa7269a15f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">verifier class ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-81c1-c358f1c171ed" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">certification error .</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8017-a37f-e3e1e4407453" class="">A certificate is now an <em>interactive relation</em>, not a semantic claim.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8063-b7b6-ec76af2510cf" class="">Definition (Verified certification relation)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8077-ba3c-d4c262df1614" class="">A verifier  is a certificate checker for  if it takes  (or  plus publicly computable derived data from ) and outputs accept/reject.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e9-8f37-d15bffacfc09" class="">Define correctness:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f9-8af0-d929357354d1" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\Pr\big[V(C,W)=1\big]\ge 1-\alpha.</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80be-a716-e40dfb217ec6" class="">Definition (Verified Certified Capacity)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-9ef0-c74ad95ecf35" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\mathsf{Cap}^{\mathrm{ver}}(\mathfrak M, \mathfrak V_T,\alpha)
:= \limsup_{n\to\infty}\ \sup_{M\in\mathfrak M}\ \frac{1}{n} H(C)
\quad \text{s.t.}\quad \exists V\in\mathfrak V_T:\Pr[V(C,W)=1]\ge 1-\alpha.
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80b0-a4f9-f004d2b5620a" class="">This is the upgraded central object.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-af51-ef46eabf4fa9"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8027-91fa-d09add302c87" class="">4) Strong corollary: your original “certified export ceiling” is now a projection</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8082-acf2-ca41fca5239f" class="">Your original framework becomes the special case where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d7-894f-fe9605d8ff61" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">checks ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8021-8ef5-e6b622260577" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">and we bound through an information term plus -slack.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-921a-cf6042d6e753" class="">This means your earlier work is not discarded—it becomes the <strong>degree-1 shadow</strong> of a bigger theory.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c3-835e-e59d264db780"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8072-965b-d43333add4b3" class="">5) Breakthrough slots (where “max power” actually happens)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-869b-c1e25e9a58b6" class="">These are the only four ways this upgrade becomes field-shifting.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8092-a0a3-e4b6c51306e6" class="">Breakthrough A: Separation theorem (new hierarchy in math)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8017-ae44-d1d213a1794c" class="">Prove existence of a family where</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8072-887e-eade71a1f799" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
I(X;C) &gt; 0\quad \text{but}\quad \mathsf{VI}_{\mathrm{poly}}(X\to C)=0.
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8092-b131-e5812ed0c5a9" class="">This is the cleanest “max power” theorem because it creates a new separation principle.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80c1-8d61-f6b32dc82004" class="">Breakthrough B: Stability–verification separation</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8008-aaa8-ca61eab4ca92" class="">For a stability class , show</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-808a-bf2d-e74068e3af3d" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\mathsf{SVI}_{\infty,\mathfrak M}(X)\ &gt;\ \mathsf{SVI}_{\mathrm{poly},\mathfrak M}(X)
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8080-910e-c65c8fe26f60" class="">Breakthrough C: Phase transition theorem</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8027-892b-c4a979a91fdb" class="">Introduce a dependence parameter  for  (Dobrushin , spectral influence, mixing). Prove a sharp threshold :</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-801d-b926-cd7f25ae6747" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\boxed{
\theta &lt; \theta^\* \Rightarrow \mathsf{Cap}^{\mathrm{ver}} &gt; 0
\qquad
\theta \ge \theta^\* \Rightarrow \mathsf{Cap}^{\mathrm{ver}} = 0
}</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8048-b11d-e2141c6d5945" class="">Breakthrough D: Tight converse + achievability (capacity theorem)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-800c-bcab-f33cc8efbeaf" class="">Produce:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8006-b5ce-d92963273fbe" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a converse upper bound on ,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f7-ad8a-faf19c32bf9e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a construction meeting it,</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80de-9fda-c1de64954e16" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">matching lower bounds.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f7-8b5c-e32b9dc02b77" class="">That is the Shannon-scale completion.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8028-b96b-ca68d3139c6c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8030-9862-ed25afb6109b" class="">6) Immediate next step (no debate, straight execution)</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8093-bf44-d4929ae798b2" class="">To proceed with “max power,” choose one flagship theorem to target:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-803c-a890-f636f419eb61" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Separation (A)</strong> — strongest conceptual payoff</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f3-bc14-dd6780a84cd2" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Phase transition (C)</strong> — strongest “classification law” payoff</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e3-b837-d0ff629d81a9" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Tight capacity (D)</strong> — strongest operational payoff</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d8-8bd4-e955e7dec00d" class="">If you say “max power” without qualifiers, the correct flagship is <strong>Separation (A)</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8013-bae5-eda1d3c5d8b1" class="">Upgrade v2: Make the theory “unframeable” by turning it into a <em>new information calculus</em> with a strict hierarchy, a conservation law, and explicit breakthrough theorem targets</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80bb-b04b-d0dd38689cdd" class="">This upgrade replaces “bounds on leakage” with a new primitive that simultaneously captures:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-802e-aead-f1a23c9a8bf9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>what exists</strong> (Shannon information),</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80d0-9d74-e351dfddef76" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>what survives stability</strong> (privacy/stability),</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8095-a299-cfd33c0781ad" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>what survives verification</strong> (computability / checkability),</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8026-afb3-d7a910e8b59f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>what survives truth constraints</strong> (certification).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8092-9298-d7f486d2e2f2" class="">It is built as a lattice + capacity + separations.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="30ec5e6f-95bd-805c-b612-cdca6da292dd"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8064-b7a6-f2ef6b934563" class="">1) Replace “information” with a 4-layer lattice</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8084-b550-ce66e19124f0" class="">Let  be data,  truth-object,  exported artifact.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80f8-958d-f9a95aba26e8" class="">Layer 0 — Raw Information</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80a4-877b-ce59e478efd0" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
\mathsf{SI}(X\to C) := I(X;C).</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="30ec5e6f-95bd-80e3-bcb8-c6e0852b9417" class="">Layer 1 — Stable Information (mechanism-restricted)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="30ec5e6f-95bd-807a-b485-d3d0e4b3800b" class="">Let  be a stability class (DP/RDP/zCDP/any divergence-bounded class):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="30ec5e6f-95bd-8014-b2b3-eb41a5365faa" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-latex" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">
